Compare SymPy and Maxima

The same questions with both assessment backends

Each question appears twice: SymPy first, then Maxima. Both copies use the same question, options, seed and variants. Choose the same variant number in both copies to compare their assessment. Answers, validation, feedback and reset are independent.

Press Validate answers to inspect each interpreted response, then Check answer to grade it. After an edit, validate again. The first assessment loads that backend; Maxima can take longer to initialize.

The native questions and graphical answers page also demonstrates native question preparation, input validation and response trees, with optional PythonChecker nodes.

Which questions need CTM?

11 questions use standard STACK features; 7 require CTM extensions. There are 3 unchanged upstream questions and 15 questions authored or adapted for CTM.

Question source and features Count Questions Stock STACK status
Unchanged upstream STACK XML 3 AlgMap-6.1, BODMAS, rounding Standard STACK features; the source XML matches the pinned upstream copies.
CTM-authored, standard STACK features 8 Rational expression, hyperbolic equivalence, numerical checks, zero-product equations, Boolean equivalence, finite lists/sets, nested collections, finite author helpers No CTM-only checker, author helper or input extension detected.
CTM PythonChecker extension 6 Determinant-one matrix, combined positive-definiteness test, separate symmetry/positivity tests, nullspace bases B and C, positive integer multiple Requires the CTM Python checker; these files do not run unchanged in stock STACK.
CTM matrix input extension, standard grading 1 Rank and pivot columns of B AlgEquiv is standard; adapt the custom resizable matrix configuration for stock STACK.
Important

PythonChecker means the same Python grading in both columns.

The six PythonChecker questions use the same supplied Python functions through Pyodide in the SymPy and Maxima copies. Every answer-test node in those six questions is a PythonChecker node: none is graded by native Maxima. The column names select the surrounding backend; they do not turn Python into Maxima.

The two nullspace questions also use CTM’s nullspace_basis(A) author helper and its resizable matrix input. The rank/pivot question uses that input extension without a PythonChecker.

These labels classify the question XML and its required features. A complete import and run in stock Moodle/STACK has not been verified here. Both columns on this page use CTM’s frontend, validation and response-tree controller. The Maxima copy delegates ordinary STACK answer-test nodes to the pinned STACK library; its PythonChecker nodes stay in Python.

Read the question-by-question source audit for the pinned evidence and extension list.

For teachers

Choose a question to try, then change the response to see which mathematical distinctions its grading preserves. The authoring guide explains how to bring your own questions to a page.

Algebraic equivalence and written form

Equivalent rational expression

Standard STACK features | CTM-authored question.

The prompt states the domain \(x\ne -n\). A cancelled fraction and the original unreduced expression both receive full credit because the question asks for any equivalent expression on that domain. A reciprocal triggers a targeted feedback branch.

SymPy

For \(x\neq-{4}\), give an expression equivalent to \(\dfrac{(x+{4})(x+{5})}{(x+{4})^2}\). You may simplify it, or give any equivalent expression on this domain.

Show source
```{.stack-question #sympy-examples-01}
#| file: questions/rational-equivalence.xml
#| question: Equivalent rational expression
#| variants: 3
#| seed: 29
#| review: true
#| feedback: true
#| label: rational-practice
#| feedback-policy: two-step-practice
```
Show STACK source
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<!-- Example question authored by the CTM team. Licensed under CC BY-SA 4.0. -->
<quiz>
  <question type="stack">
    <name>
      <text>Equivalent rational expression</text>
    </name>
    <questiontext format="html">
      <text>&lt;p&gt;For \(x\neq-{@n@}\), give an expression equivalent to \(\dfrac{(x+{@n@})(x+{@m@})}{(x+{@n@})^2}\). You may simplify it, or give any equivalent expression on this domain.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;[[input:ans]] [[validation:ans]] [[feedback:prt1]]&lt;/p&gt;</text>
    </questiontext>
    <generalfeedback format="html">
      <text>&lt;p&gt;Cancel one common factor of \(x+{@n@}\) (valid when \(x\neq-{@n@}\)) to obtain \({@ta@}\).&lt;/p&gt;</text>
    </generalfeedback>
    <defaultgrade>1</defaultgrade>
    <penalty>0.1</penalty>
    <hidden>0</hidden>
    <idnumber/>
    <stackversion>
      <text>2021072200</text>
    </stackversion>
    <questionvariables>
      <text>n:rand(3)+2;
m:n+rand(3)+1;
ta:(x+m)/(x+n);</text>
    </questionvariables>
    <specificfeedback format="html">
      <text>&lt;p&gt;[[feedback:prt1]]&lt;/p&gt;</text>
    </specificfeedback>
    <questionnote format="moodle_auto_format">
      <text>Rational identity: (x+n)(x+m)/(x+n)^2 = ta, for x != -n</text>
    </questionnote>
    <questiondescription format="moodle_auto_format">
      <text/>
    </questiondescription>
    <questionsimplify>1</questionsimplify>
    <assumepositive>0</assumepositive>
    <assumereal>0</assumereal>
    <scientificnotation>*10</scientificnotation>
    <multiplicationsign>none</multiplicationsign>
    <sqrtsign>1</sqrtsign>
    <complexno>i</complexno>
    <inversetrig>cos-1</inversetrig>
    <logicsymbol>lang</logicsymbol>
    <matrixparens>[</matrixparens>
    <isbroken>0</isbroken>
    <variantsselectionseed/>
    <input>
      <name>ans</name>
      <type>algebraic</type>
      <tans>ta</tans>
      <boxsize>18</boxsize>
      <strictsyntax>1</strictsyntax>
      <insertstars>0</insertstars>
      <syntaxhint/>
      <syntaxattribute>0</syntaxattribute>
      <forbidwords/>
      <allowwords/>
      <forbidfloat>1</forbidfloat>
      <requirelowestterms>0</requirelowestterms>
      <checkanswertype>1</checkanswertype>
      <mustverify>1</mustverify>
      <showvalidation>1</showvalidation>
      <options/>
    </input>
    <prt>
      <name>prt1</name>
      <value>1.0000000</value>
      <autosimplify>0</autosimplify>
      <feedbackstyle>1</feedbackstyle>
      <feedbackvariables>
        <text/>
      </feedbackvariables>
      <node>
        <name>0</name>
        <description/>
        <answertest>AlgEquiv</answertest>
        <sans>ans</sans>
        <tans>ta</tans>
        <testoptions/>
        <quiet>0</quiet>
        <truescoremode>=</truescoremode>
        <truescore>1.0000000</truescore>
        <truepenalty/>
        <truenextnode>-1</truenextnode>
        <trueanswernote>prt1-0-T</trueanswernote>
        <truefeedback format="html">
          <text>&lt;p&gt;Correct on the stated domain.&lt;/p&gt;</text>
        </truefeedback>
        <falsescoremode>=</falsescoremode>
        <falsescore>0.0000000</falsescore>
        <falsepenalty/>
        <falsenextnode>1</falsenextnode>
        <falseanswernote>prt1-0-F</falseanswernote>
        <falsefeedback format="html">
          <text/>
        </falsefeedback>
      </node>
      <node>
        <name>1</name>
        <description/>
        <answertest>AlgEquiv</answertest>
        <sans>ans</sans>
        <tans>(x+n)/(x+m)</tans>
        <testoptions/>
        <quiet>0</quiet>
        <truescoremode>=</truescoremode>
        <truescore>0.0000000</truescore>
        <truepenalty/>
        <truenextnode>-1</truenextnode>
        <trueanswernote>prt1-1-T</trueanswernote>
        <truefeedback format="html">
          <text>&lt;p&gt;You inverted the fraction. Cancel a shared factor instead.&lt;/p&gt;</text>
        </truefeedback>
        <falsescoremode>=</falsescoremode>
        <falsescore>0.0000000</falsescore>
        <falsepenalty/>
        <falsenextnode>-1</falsenextnode>
        <falseanswernote>prt1-1-F</falseanswernote>
        <falsefeedback format="html">
          <text>&lt;p&gt;Cancel one factor in the numerator against one in the squared denominator.&lt;/p&gt;</text>
        </falsefeedback>
      </node>
    </prt>
    <deployedseed>17</deployedseed>
    <deployedseed>29</deployedseed>
    <deployedseed>47</deployedseed>
    <qtest>
      <testcase>1</testcase>
      <description>Simplified expression</description>
      <testinput>
        <name>ans</name>
        <value>(x+m)/(x+n)</value>
      </testinput>
      <expected>
        <name>prt1</name>
        <expectedscore>1.0000000</expectedscore>
        <expectedpenalty>0.0000000</expectedpenalty>
        <expectedanswernote>prt1-0-T</expectedanswernote>
      </expected>
    </qtest>
    <qtest>
      <testcase>2</testcase>
      <description>Unsimplified but equivalent expression</description>
      <testinput>
        <name>ans</name>
        <value>(x+n)*(x+m)/(x+n)^2</value>
      </testinput>
      <expected>
        <name>prt1</name>
        <expectedscore>1.0000000</expectedscore>
        <expectedpenalty>0.0000000</expectedpenalty>
        <expectedanswernote>prt1-0-T</expectedanswernote>
      </expected>
    </qtest>
    <qtest>
      <testcase>3</testcase>
      <description>Reciprocal misconception</description>
      <testinput>
        <name>ans</name>
        <value>(x+n)/(x+m)</value>
      </testinput>
      <expected>
        <name>prt1</name>
        <expectedscore>0.0000000</expectedscore>
        <expectedpenalty>0.1000000</expectedpenalty>
        <expectedanswernote>prt1-0-F | prt1-1-T</expectedanswernote>
      </expected>
    </qtest>
  </question>
</quiz>
Maxima

For \(x\neq-{4}\), give an expression equivalent to \(\dfrac{(x+{4})(x+{5})}{(x+{4})^2}\). You may simplify it, or give any equivalent expression on this domain.

Show source
::: {.stack-maxima #maxima-examples-01}
#| file: questions/rational-equivalence.xml
#| question: Equivalent rational expression
#| variants: 3
#| seed: 29
#| review: true
#| feedback: true
#| label: rational-practice
#| feedback-policy: two-step-practice
:::

Download the rational question

Its AI Feedback first asks a guiding question and then offers a worked explanation. The teaching-policy setup shows how to choose a sequence for your own course.

An imported STACK question

Standard STACK features | unchanged upstream STACK XML.

This unchanged Moodle export asks for a particular written form. Its author tests remain in the XML, so you can inspect how EqualComAss grades the response. If an imported question uses an unsupported feature, the site build identifies it for the author.

SymPy

Simplify the following expression by collecting together like terms:
\[{10}x+{8}y-{6}x-{7}y+5.\]

Show source
```{.stack-question #sympy-examples-02}
#| file: questions/upstream/algmap-6-1.xml
#| question: AlgMap-6.1
#| variants: 2
#| seed: 17
#| review: true
#| feedback: true
#| context: none
```
Show STACK source
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<quiz>
  <question type="stack">
    <name>
      <text>AlgMap-6.1</text>
    </name>
    <questiontext format="html">
      <text><![CDATA[<p>Simplify the following expression by collecting together like terms: <br /> \[{@a@}x+{@b@}y-{@c@}x-{@d@}y+5.\] <br /> [[input:ans]] [[validation:ans]]</p>]]></text>
    </questiontext>
    <generalfeedback format="html">
      <text><![CDATA[<p>In order to collect like terms simply pull out the common factors (the x's and y's in this example) like so: <br /> \[ = {@a@}x+{@b@}y-{@c@}x-{@d@}y+5,\] <br /> \[ = \left({@a@}-{@c@}\right)x+\left({@b@}-{@d@}\right)y+5,\] <br /> \[ = {@ta@}.\]</p>]]></text>
    </generalfeedback>
    <defaultgrade>1</defaultgrade>
    <penalty>0.1</penalty>
    <hidden>0</hidden>
    <idnumber/>
    <stackversion>
      <text>2019121800</text>
    </stackversion>
    <questionvariables>
      <text>a:rand(9)+2;
b:rand(9)+2;
c:rand(9)+2;
d:rand(9)+2;
ta:ev(a*x+b*y-c*x-d*y+5,simp);</text>
    </questionvariables>
    <specificfeedback format="html">
      <text><![CDATA[<p>[[feedback:prt1]]</p>]]></text>
    </specificfeedback>
    <questionnote format="moodle_auto_format">
      <text>\[{@a@}x+{@b@}y-{@c@}x-{@d@}y+5 = {@ta@}.\]</text>
    </questionnote>
    <questiondescription format="moodle_auto_format">
      <text/>
    </questiondescription>
    <questionsimplify>1</questionsimplify>
    <assumepositive>0</assumepositive>
    <assumereal>0</assumereal>
    <scientificnotation>*10</scientificnotation>
    <multiplicationsign>none</multiplicationsign>
    <sqrtsign>1</sqrtsign>
    <complexno>i</complexno>
    <inversetrig>cos-1</inversetrig>
    <logicsymbol>lang</logicsymbol>
    <matrixparens>[</matrixparens>
    <isbroken>0</isbroken>
    <variantsselectionseed/>
    <input>
      <name>ans</name>
      <type>algebraic</type>
      <tans>ta</tans>
      <boxsize>15</boxsize>
      <strictsyntax>1</strictsyntax>
      <insertstars>0</insertstars>
      <syntaxhint/>
      <syntaxattribute>0</syntaxattribute>
      <forbidwords>ratsimp</forbidwords>
      <allowwords/>
      <forbidfloat>1</forbidfloat>
      <requirelowestterms>1</requirelowestterms>
      <checkanswertype>1</checkanswertype>
      <mustverify>1</mustverify>
      <showvalidation>1</showvalidation>
      <options/>
    </input>
    <prt>
      <name>prt1</name>
      <value>1.0000000</value>
      <autosimplify>0</autosimplify>
      <feedbackstyle>1</feedbackstyle>
      <feedbackvariables>
        <text/>
      </feedbackvariables>
      <node>
        <name>0</name>
        <description/>
        <answertest>EqualComAss</answertest>
        <sans>ans</sans>
        <tans>ta</tans>
        <testoptions/>
        <quiet>0</quiet>
        <truescoremode>=</truescoremode>
        <truescore>1.0000000</truescore>
        <truepenalty/>
        <truenextnode>-1</truenextnode>
        <trueanswernote>1-0-T </trueanswernote>
        <truefeedback format="html">
          <text/>
        </truefeedback>
        <falsescoremode>=</falsescoremode>
        <falsescore>0.0000000</falsescore>
        <falsepenalty/>
        <falsenextnode>-1</falsenextnode>
        <falseanswernote>1-0-F </falseanswernote>
        <falsefeedback format="html">
          <text/>
        </falsefeedback>
      </node>
    </prt>
    <deployedseed>1533443292</deployedseed>
    <deployedseed>1987024481</deployedseed>

Hyperbolic equivalence

Standard STACK features | CTM-authored question.

Find another expression for a logistic function. This question accepts a supported equivalent expression using hyperbolic functions, such as tanh. It checks mathematical equivalence rather than requiring one written form.

SymPy

Give an expression equivalent to \(1/(1+\exp(-2x))\). You may use hyperbolic functions.

Numerical answers and precision

Three numerical checks

Standard STACK features | CTM-authored question.

These fields demonstrate absolute tolerance, relative tolerance, and decimal-place precision. The final field distinguishes 17.5 from 17.50, because the written precision is part of the task. Their response trees contribute 30%, 40%, and 30% of the total.

For a measured quantity, absolute tolerance compares \(|x-a|\) to a fixed bound. Relative tolerance compares it to a fraction of the reference value. Written decimal places express the precision of a reported measurement.

SymPy

A rectangle is \({7}\) cm long and \({5}\) cm wide. A right triangle has those same perpendicular side lengths.

  1. Find the rectangle perimeter in cm, within \(0.25\) cm.
  2. Find the triangle area in cm², within \(2\%\) of the exact value.
  3. Write the triangle area to exactly two decimal places (include the final zero).
Show source
```{.stack-question #sympy-examples-04}
#| file: questions/numerical-measurement.xml
#| question: Rectangle and triangle measurements
#| seed: 47
#| review: true
#| feedback: true
#| context: measurement-context
```

Maxima

A rectangle is \({7}\) cm long and \({5}\) cm wide. A right triangle has those same perpendicular side lengths.

  1. Find the rectangle perimeter in cm, within \(0.25\) cm.
  2. Find the triangle area in cm², within \(2\%\) of the exact value.
  3. Write the triangle area to exactly two decimal places (include the final zero).
Show source

#| review: true
#| feedback: true
#| context: measurement-context

A right triangle has those same perpendicular side lengths.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ol&gt;&lt;li&gt;Find the rectangle perimeter in cm, within \(0.25\) cm.     <generalfeedback format="html">
      <text><![CDATA[<p>The BODMAS rule tells us to perform the multiplication before the addition. Thus</p>

<p>We then perform addition to give the final answer</p>

Original rounding question

Standard STACK features | unchanged upstream STACK XML.

This unchanged question has several answer fields and checks the number of reported decimal places. Its author tests are visible in review mode.

SymPy

Exercise

Write down each of these numbers rounded to 3 decimal places:

  1. \({\frac{222353}{250000}}\)
  2. \({- \frac{1543}{10000}}\)
  3. \({\frac{847981}{1000000}}\)
  4. \(−0.5555,\)
  5. \(45.6789\)
  6. \({\frac{10001}{5000}}\)

Equations and logic

Zero product equations

Standard STACK features | CTM-authored question.

Rewrite \(ab=0\) as alternatives joined by or. Here AlgEquiv checks the solutions represented by polynomial equations, including repeated-root information; it is not simply comparing the strings on either side.

SymPy

Express \(ab=0\) as a disjunction of polynomial equations. Write or between the alternatives.

Show source
```{.stack-question #sympy-examples-07}
#| file: questions/zero-product.xml
#| question: Zero product equations
#| seed: 17
#| review: true
```

Boolean equivalence

Standard STACK features | CTM-authored question.

Use De Morgan’s law to rewrite not(a and b) using or. In this question a and b are propositions, and the checker compares their truth values for every assignment in its supported finite domain.

SymPy

Rewrite the negation of a and b using or. Here a and b are propositions.

Collections

Finite lists and sets

Standard STACK features | CTM-authored question.

Compare an ordered list with an unordered set. Lists retain positions and repetitions; sets ignore order and repeated mathematical members. One field also shows feedback about extra, missing or repeated set entries.

SymPy

Use square brackets for an ordered list and braces for a set. Here a = \({6}\).

Enter the list \({\left[ 6, \ 2 x\right]}\):

Enter the set \({\left\{6, 2 x\right\}}\):

Enter the same set again; this check explains duplicates and missing/extra elements:

Nested collections and written form

Standard STACK features | CTM-authored question.

Try a list containing another list and a set. The task keeps nested boundaries and list order; a separate written-form check can distinguish repeated entries or different arithmetic forms even when values agree.

SymPy

Use lists to preserve order and sets to ignore order. Here a = \({6}\).

Reproduce the nested value \({\left[\left[6, 2x\right], \left\{1, \left\{2, 3\right\}\right\}, \left[\right]\right]}\):

Reproduce the family of collections \({\left\{\left\{1, 2\right\}, \left[6, 2x\right]\right\}}\):

Reproduce the written form (reorder only) \({\left\{\left\{x + x, 2, 2\right\}, \left[1, x + 1\right]\right\}}\):

Show source
```{.stack-question #sympy-examples-10}
#| file: questions/nested-collections.xml
#| question: Nested finite collections
#| seed: 17
#| review: true
```

Maxima

Use lists to preserve order and sets to ignore order. Here a = \({6}\).

Reproduce the nested value \({\left[\left[6, 2x\right], \left\{1, \left\{2, 3\right\}\right\}, \left[\right]\right]}\):

Reproduce the family of collections \({\left\{\left\{1, 2\right\}, \left[6, 2x\right]\right\}}\):

Reproduce the written form (reorder only) \({\left\{\left\{x + x, 2, 2\right\}, \left[1, x + 1\right]\right\}}\):

Finite author helpers

Standard STACK features | CTM-authored question. listify, setify, union, intersection, setdifference, member, subsetp and cardinality are ordinary Maxima/STACK functions, not CTM extensions.

This question’s list and set are calculated from author variables. Learners still enter ordinary list and set literals; the helper functions are for preparing question values, not new learner syntax.

Matrix conditions

Find a nonidentity matrix with determinant one

CTM extension required: PythonChecker. Python grades this question in both columns; Maxima executes no native answer-test node.

Enter any integer \(2\times2\) matrix with determinant 1, except the identity. Each of the three conditions earns one third of the score: integer entries, determinant one, and nonidentity. You are finding a matrix that satisfies conditions, rather than copying a displayed model answer.

SymPy

Enter any integer \(2\times2\) matrix \(A\ne I_2\) with \(\det(A)=1\).

Show source

```{.stack-question #sympy-examples-12}
#| file: questions/determinant-one.xml

#| python-checkers: questions/matrix-checkers.py
#| seed: 17
#| review: true
#| feedback: true
#| context: none
```

Download XML

The question uses three normal response trees, one per condition. Named functions in the matrix checker file check integer entries, determinant one and nonidentity; see which test to choose.

A positive definite matrix

CTM extension required: PythonChecker. Python grades this question in both columns; Maxima executes no native answer-test node.

Give any real symmetric or complex Hermitian positive definite \(2\times2\) matrix, using integers or exact fractions for both real and imaginary parts. Enter %i for \(i\). The identity, \(\begin{pmatrix}2&1\\1&2\end{pmatrix}\) and matrix([2,%i],[-%i,2]) all pass. The combined is_hpd checker makes one decision and its response tree awards full credit only when both Hermitian symmetry and positivity hold. Compare this with the separate-check question below; the teacher explanation shows the exact mathematical test.

SymPy

Give any real symmetric or complex Hermitian positive definite \(2\times2\) matrix. Use integers or exact fractions for real and imaginary parts; enter %i for \(i\).

    </questiontext>
    <generalfeedback format="html">
      <text>&lt;p&gt;The checker uses Sylvester’s criterion to test positivity with exact arithmetic. Full credit requires both Hermitian symmetry and positivity. For real entries, Hermitian symmetry means ordinary symmetry.&lt;/p&gt;</text>
    </generalfeedback>
    <defaultgrade>1</defaultgrade>
    <penalty>0.1</penalty>

      <name>ansm</name>

<!-- Project example, CC BY-SA 4.0. Uses matrix-checkers.py selected in Quarto. -->
<quiz>
  <question type="stack">
    <name>
      <text>A positive definite matrix</text>
    </name>

      <text />
    </questionvariables>
    <questionsimplify>1</questionsimplify>
    <questiontext format="html">
      <text>&lt;p&gt;Give any real symmetric or complex Hermitian positive definite \(2\times2\) matrix. Use integers or exact fractions for real and imaginary parts; enter %i for \(i\).&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;[[input:ansm]] [[validation:ansm]] [[feedback:result]]&lt;/p&gt;</text>
    </questiontext>
    <generalfeedback format="html">
      <text>&lt;p&gt;The checker uses Sylvester’s criterion to test positivity with exact arithmetic. Full credit requires both Hermitian symmetry and positivity. For real entries, Hermitian symmetry means ordinary symmetry.&lt;/p&gt;</text>

Download question XML · View the supplied matrix checkers

Hermitian symmetry and positive definiteness separately

CTM extension required: PythonChecker. Python grades this question in both columns; Maxima executes no native answer-test node.

This version checks Hermitian symmetry and positivity separately, awarding half the marks for each. The checker uses Sylvester’s criterion. See the four-case comparison to compare the two scoring choices.

SymPy

Give any real symmetric or complex Hermitian positive definite \(2\times2\) matrix. Use integers or exact fractions for real and imaginary parts; enter %i for \(i\).

Half the marks are for Hermitian symmetry and half for positivity of the quadratic form.

Hermitian symmetry:

Positivity:

Show source

```{.stack-question #sympy-examples-14}
#| file: questions/symmetric-positive-definite-prt.xml
#| question: Hermitian symmetry and positive definiteness separately
#| python-checkers: questions/matrix-checkers.py
#| review: true
```

Rank and bases

These three questions adapt a linear algebra course’s rank and null-space exercises.

Rank and pivot columns: ordinary STACK answer tests

CTM input extension required: resizable matrix. Grading uses standard AlgEquiv; no PythonChecker is used. This XML’s custom matrix sizing needs adaptation to stock STACK’s matrix or varmatrix input configuration.

This question uses AlgEquiv and two equally weighted response trees. The rank and the complete ordered pivot list each earn half the total.

SymPy

Find the rank and the pivot-column numbers of \(B={\begin{pmatrix}1 & 0 & 1 \\ 0 & 1 & 1 \\ 1 & 1 & 2\end{pmatrix}}\). List the pivot-column numbers in increasing order, one number per column. Choose the number of columns yourself.

Rank:

Pivot columns:

A basis for the null space of B

CTM extension required: PythonChecker. Python grades this question in both columns; Maxima executes no native answer-test node. It also requires CTM’s nullspace_basis(A) author helper and bounded resizable matrix input. Both need adaptation for stock STACK.

Put one proposed basis vector in each column. A basis is not a unique matrix: changing a vector’s nonzero scale can still give a correct answer. Choose the number of columns before validating. The checker uses the original matrix \(B\) to define the space, so another independent basis is accepted.

SymPy

Give a basis for the null space of \(B={\begin{pmatrix}1 & 0 & 1 \\ 0 & 1 & 1 \\ 1 & 1 & 2\end{pmatrix}}\), with one vector in each column. Any basis is accepted. Choose the number of columns yourself; use exact fractions.

Show source

```{.stack-question #sympy-examples-16}
#| file: questions/course-b-nullspace.xml
#| question: A basis for the null space of B
#| python-checkers: questions/matrix-checkers.py
#| seed: 17
#| review: true
```

Download XML

A basis for the null space of C

CTM extension required: PythonChecker. Python grades this question in both columns; Maxima executes no native answer-test node. It also requires CTM’s nullspace_basis(A) author helper and bounded resizable matrix input. Both need adaptation for stock STACK.

Here you may reorder or combine basis vectors, provided your columns remain independent and span the null space of \(C\). Entry-by-entry equality with one model matrix would reject many correct answers. The checker uses the original matrix \(C\) to define the target space.

SymPy

Give a basis for the null space of \(C={\begin{pmatrix}1 & 2 & 3 \\ 2 & 4 & 6\end{pmatrix}}\), with one vector in each column. Any basis is accepted. Choose the number of columns yourself; use exact fractions.

Download XML

For both basis questions, green marks a nonzero vector in the target space when the valid submitted vectors are independent. A green family can still be incomplete. Yellow marks every member of a dependent valid family; red marks a zero vector or one outside the space. The whole answer earns credit only when it is a complete basis. Colors explain the result but do not award partial marks. Editing or resizing clears them.

Read how to author a basis question or configure its matrix input.

A custom scalar rule

Custom Python checker

CTM extension required: PythonChecker. Python grades this question in both columns; Maxima executes no native answer-test node.

Enter any positive integer multiple of the displayed number. A named PythonChecker function decides whether the submitted integer has the required property; the question’s ordinary response tree awards credit. See the author API.

Where does an answer go?

Read Data flow for the typed-input and JSXGraph walkthroughs, with expanded developer details and diagrams of validation and grading.

Startup and response times

Frozen public measurements: milestone 3

The hybrid Maxima worker now defers native startup until its first ordinary Check. On the measured build, first Validate took 5.99 s, while first Check took 38.45 s including 38.40 s of deferred Maxima/STACK initialization. Later Checks took 42 ms. Validation still uses Python in both columns. A PythonChecker-only question does not start Maxima.

These frozen measurements describe deployed source 15fa33299d68d64e122a2661a2868feb30130edb, measured on 22 October 2026 by benchmark attempt 2. Subsequent page edits do not rerun or relabel this batch. The workload is the first rational-expression question, variant 1, with the same accepted answer in both columns.

Values are median / minimum / maximum. First-call rows have 3 samples per backend; warm rows have 15 samples per backend, five calls in each of three fresh processes.

Interval SymPy Hybrid Maxima
First Validate: UI (s) 5.901 / 5.899 / 5.944 5.992 / 5.931 / 6.315
Worker initialization (s) 5.715 / 5.710 / 5.755 5.805 / 5.742 / 6.131
First Validate: worker RPC (ms) 186.53 / 183.96 / 187.32 184.38 / 182.02 / 187.16
Warm Validate: UI (ms) 11.79 / 9.77 / 12.33 12.15 / 11.25 / 12.45
First Check after validation: UI (s) 0.011 / 0.011 / 0.012 38.446 / 38.178 / 38.964
First Check: worker RPC (s) 0.009 / 0.009 / 0.010 38.444 / 38.175 / 38.962
Warm Check: UI (ms) 11.71 / 10.02 / 12.98 42.26 / 40.49 / 46.85
Warm Check: worker RPC (ms) 10.80 / 9.06 / 12.17 41.41 / 39.71 / 45.85

For hybrid Maxima, deferred native initialization alone was 38.401 / 38.129 / 38.919 s (3 samples). It is included in first Check and its worker RPC; these intervals must not be added together. First Check follows six validations, so it is the first native grade, not a cold page or a cold Python worker.

UI time runs from the actual button click to the verdict DOM update. Worker RPC time runs from the real worker message to its response. Initialization is recorded separately and is included in the relevant first UI operation. Page navigation, HTML loading, isolation bootstrap, answer entry and later MathJax painting are outside these button intervals.

The native assessment page measures the different path where native STACK also prepares questions, validates inputs and runs response trees. Its warm grading is more expensive than this page’s hybrid answer-test delegation; the two controllers should not be treated as identical workloads.

Method and limits

The runner used Chromium 141.0.7390.37 on GitHub-hosted Ubuntu, 4 virtual CPUs. Each backend had three fresh Chromium processes, profiles and workers. Each started with Validate and zero prior assessment RPCs, then five warm validations, its first grade and five warm grades. Backend order alternated. OS and CDN caches were not cleared; provenance fetches can warm CDN caches.

The benchmark verified the exact final browser navigation HTML and public asset fingerprints, then checked HTML/assets again after the samples. See the frozen summaries, sample times, hashes and provenance and the timing evidence guide. This is one accepted answer on one runner, not a device/network benchmark, a confidence interval or evidence of a general performance advantage. Ordinary functional builds leave these statistics frozen.

Historical milestone 2 baseline

The original eager-start build waited for Python and Maxima together: first Maxima Validate was 43.28 s, including 43.10 s of worker initialization; warm Validate was about 12.4 ms. SymPy first Validate was 5.95 s. Those are historical values from run 39759255662, with its original report. They used an earlier build and run, so the change between batches is not a controlled speedup estimate.

Later questions, variant changes and Reset reuse the loaded worker. Reloading creates new workers; cached files can reduce download work while runtime initialization remains.

For developers

Use the authoring guide for imports, question options, tests and AI Feedback policies. The Python answer-test guide explains the basis and property choices and gives the complete positive-definiteness checker walkthrough. The questions above link to their XML; the positive-definiteness question also links to its Python file. The rational activity chooses the two-step-practice policy, while other feedback activities inherit a four-step sequence; see policy setup. Run authored tests and inspect alternate correct answers before publishing.

Sources

The determinant-one question adapts a math-exercise custom-assessment example; its provenance record gives the adaptation and license. The rank/pivot and null-space questions adapt INGMat 3A week 3 exercises under CC BY-SA 4.0; their source record identifies the exact originals. The unchanged imported questions come from the STACK collection, with individual source and license details.
